\{T(t/k_{j})\}^{k_{j}}-e^{-iH^{F}t}
.
It can be calculated based on the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff (BCH) formula like
e^{At}e^{Bt}=e^{(A+B)t+[B,A]t^{2}+\order{t^3}}
.
The order-
p_{0}
(
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
) BCH formula for the time-independent PF
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
is defined by

\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\text{BCH}}(t)=\exp\left(-iH^{F}t-i\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}t^{q}\right)=\exp\left(-i\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}t^{q}\right),

(220)

where the order-
q
operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
is defined by
\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{F}=H^{F}
and

\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}=(-i)^{q-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{11},\cdots,q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}\geq 0;\\
q_{11}+\cdots+q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}=q\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{v=1}^{V_{p}}\prod_{\tilde{\gamma}=1}^{2\Gamma}\frac{(\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}})^{q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}}}{q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}!}\right)\phi_{q}(\underbrace{\tilde{H}_{V_{p}2\Gamma},\cdots,\tilde{H}_{V_{p}2\Gamma}}_{q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}},\cdots,\underbrace{\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}},\cdots,\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}}_{q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}},\cdots,\underbrace{\tilde{H}_{11},\cdots,\tilde{H}_{11}}_{q_{11}}),

(221)

\phi_{q}(H_{1},\cdots,H_{q})=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{S}_{q}}\frac{(-1)^{d_{\sigma}}d_{\sigma}!(q-1-d_{\sigma})!}{q^{2}(q-1)!}[H_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,[H_{\sigma(q-1)},H_{\sigma(q)}]]],

(222)

for
q\geq 2
.
The symbol
\mathcal{S}_{q}
is the symmetric group of
q
elements and
d_{\sigma}
is the number of adjacent pairs such that
\sigma(i+1)<\sigma(i)
, i.e.,
d_{\sigma}=|\{i\,|\,\sigma(i+1)<\sigma(i)\}|
.
We have
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}=0
for
q=2,3,\cdots,p
due to the order condition of the PF,
\bar{T}^{F}(t)=e^{-iH^{F}t}+\order{t^{p+1}}
.
At the same time, the order condition for the order-
p_{0}
BCH formula implies
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\text{BCH}}(t)=\bar{T}^{F}(t)+\order{t^{p_0+1}}
.
Each order-
q
term
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
in the BCH formula has the following property.

Lemma C1
.

Each term in the BCH formula,
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
by Eq. (
221
) is written in the form of

\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{Add}_{m}\otimes\bar{\Phi}_{q,m},\quad\bar{\Phi}_{q,m}=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\differential t\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)e^{im\omega t}.

(223)

The operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
is bounded by

\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(t)}\leq(V_{p})^{q}\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},q-1}(t)\leq(q-1)!(V_{p})^{q}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q-1}Ng,

(224)

where
\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},q}(t)
is defined by Eq. (
45
).

Proof.—

By the definition
\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{F}=H^{F}
, we have
\bar{\Phi}_{1}(t)=H(t)
and
\norm{\bar{\Phi}_1(t)}\leq Ng
, which completes the proof for
q=1
.
We focus on the case with
q\geq 2
.
Equations (
221
) and (
222
) say that
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
is composed of nested commutators among
\{H_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{Add}}\}
and
H^{\mathrm{LP}}
.
Since they have the form like Eq. (
98
) as discussed in Section
V.2
, the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
is also written in the same form.
We next evaluate the upper bound on
\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(t)}
.
Each
q
-fold nested commutator
[\tilde{H}_{v_{q}\tilde{\gamma}_{q}},\cdots,[\tilde{H}_{v_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}},\tilde{H}_{v_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}]]
in the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
comes from the permutation
\sigma\in S_{q}
such that
(\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(q))=(v_{q}\tilde{\gamma}_{q},\cdots,v_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1})
under fixed indices
q_{11},\cdots,q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}
satisfying
q_{11}+\cdots+q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}=q
.
Let us denote the set of such permutations by
S_{q}^{\prime}
, and then we have
|S_{q}^{\prime}|=q_{11}!\cdots q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}!
.
The absolute value of the nested commutator
[\tilde{H}_{v_{q}\tilde{\gamma}_{q}},\cdots,[\tilde{H}_{v_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}},\tilde{H}_{v_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}]]
is bounded by

\left|\left(\prod_{v=1}^{V_{p}}\prod_{\tilde{\gamma}=1}^{2\Gamma}\frac{(\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}})^{q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}}}{q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}!}\right)\sum_{\sigma\in S_{q}^{\prime}}\frac{(-1)^{d_{\sigma}}d_{\sigma}!(q-1-d_{\sigma})!}{q^{2}(q-1)!}\right|\leq\frac{1}{q_{11}!\cdots q_{V_{p}2\Gamma}!}\frac{|S_{q}^{\prime}|}{q^{2}}=\frac{1}{q^{2}}.

(225)

When we move to the representation in the time domain, the commutator
\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}}
is replaced by
\bar{D}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}(t)
as shown in Lemma
9
.
We should be careful of the parity of the index
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
.
For an odd integer
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}=2\gamma_{1}-1
, the nested commutator
[\tilde{H}_{v_{q}\tilde{\gamma}_{q}},\cdots,[\tilde{H}_{v_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}},\tilde{H}_{v_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}]]
gives rise to the term
\prod_{q^{\prime}=2}^{p}\bar{D}_{v_{q^{\prime}}\tilde{\gamma}_{q^{\prime}}}(t)H_{\gamma_{1}}(t)
in the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
following Eq. (
93
).
On the other hand, when
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
is even, the commutator
[\tilde{H}_{v_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}},\tilde{H}_{v_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}]
survives only for an odd integer
\tilde{\gamma}_{2}=2\gamma_{2}-1
, and then it gives
i\derivative{t}H_{\gamma_{2}}(t)
according to Eq. (
94
).
Therefore, the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}(t)
is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q^{2}}\sum_{v_{1}=1}^{V_{p}}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{v_{2}\tilde{\gamma}_{2},\cdots,v_{q}\tilde{\gamma}_{q}=11}^{V_{p}2\Gamma}\norm{\left(\prod_{q'=2}^p \bar{D}_{v_{q'}\tilde{\gamma}_{q'}}(t)\right) H_{\gamma_1}(t)}

(226)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{q^{2}}\sum_{v_{1}=1}^{V_{p}}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{v_{2}=1}^{V_{p}}\sum_{\gamma_{2}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{v_{3}\tilde{\gamma}_{3},\cdots,v_{q}\tilde{\gamma}_{q}=11}^{V_{p}2\Gamma}\norm{\left(\prod_{q'=3}^p \bar{D}_{v_{q'}\tilde{\gamma}_{q'}}(t)\right) \dv{t} H_{\gamma_2}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{q^{2}}(V_{p})^{q}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{p}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\left( \prod_{q=2}^p \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}(t)\right) H_{\gamma_1}(t)}\leq(V_{p})^{q}\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},q-1}(t),

which completes the proof of the first inequality in Eq. (
224
).
The second inequality in Eq. (
224
) immediately comes from Theorem
14
.
\quad\square

We next consider the locality, extensiveness, and the strength of time-dependency of the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
as a Hamiltonian.
The operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
is written in the form of

\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)=\sum_{X\subset\Lambda}h_{X}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q}}(t),

(227)

where each
h_{X}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q}}(t)
denotes a local operator nontrivially acting on the domain
X
.
In a similar manner to Eq. (
129
), we define the extensiveness
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
by a number such that the relation,

\max_{i\in\Lambda}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\bar{\Phi}_q}(\tau)}\leq g(\bar{\Phi}_{q}),

(228)

holds.
We also define the strength of time-dependency
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
by a number such that the relation,

\max_{i\in\Lambda}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{\dv[q']{\tau} h_X^{\bar{\Phi}_q}(\tau)}\leq[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})]^{q^{\prime}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q}),

(229)

holds for every
q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.
We characterize these quantities based on the counterparts of the Hamiltonian
H(t)
as follows.

Lemma C2
.

The operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
defined by Eq. (
223
) is at most
qk
-local.
In addition, its extensiveness and strength of time-dependency can be chosen as

g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})=q!(V_{p})^{q}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q-1}g,\quad f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})=qf,

(230)

which satisfy the relations Eqs. (
228
) and (
229
).

Proof.—

The case with
q=1
is trivial and we consider
q\geq 2
.
The operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
is composed of
q
-fold nested commutators among
H_{\gamma}(t)
and their time derivative.
Since each
H_{\gamma}(t)
is
k
-local and the time derivative preserves the locality, the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
is at most
qk
-local.

We evaluate the quantities
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
and
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
.
Let us expand the operator
\prod_{q^{\prime}=2}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)H_{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)
by

\left[\prod_{q^{\prime}=2}^{q}\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)\right]H_{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)=\sum_{X\subset\Lambda}\bar{h}_{X}^{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}}(\tau),

(231)

with a local term
\bar{h}_{X}^{\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{q}}(\tau)
nontrivially acting on a domain
X
.
The discussion on the locality says that
\bar{h}_{X}^{\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{q}}(\tau)
can be nonzero only for a domain
X
such that
|X|\leq qk
.
As well as the discussion in the proof of Lemma
C1
, the local terms in
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(t)
is covered by
(V_{p})^{q}
copies of the nested commutators among
\{H_{\gamma}(t)\}
and their time derivative with a coefficient smaller than
2/q^{2}\leq 1
, which leads to the relations,

\displaystyle\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\bar{\Phi}_q}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)},

(232)

\displaystyle\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{\dv[q']{\tau} h_X^{\bar{\Phi}_q}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\dv[q']{\tau} \bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)}.

(233)

We aim to find the quantities
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
and
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
such that the inequalities

(V_{p})^{q}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)}\leq g(\bar{\Phi}_{q}),\quad(V_{p})^{q}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\dv[q']{\tau} \bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)}\leq[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})]^{q^{\prime}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})

(234)

are satisfied.

We determine the quantities
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
and
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
recursively.
Let us consider the extensiveness for
q+1
.
The local terms of Eq. (
231
) for
q+1
are composed of the commutators
[h_{X^{\prime}}^{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau),\bar{h}_{X}^{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}}(\tau)]
, which come from the application of
\bar{D}_{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau)
for
\gamma_{q+1}=1,\cdots,\Gamma
, and the time derivative
\Gamma\derivative{\tau}\bar{h}_{X}^{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}}(\tau)
, which comes from that for
\gamma_{q+1}=\Gamma+1
.
As a result, the extensiveness is evaluated by

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,X^{\prime}\subset\Lambda;\\
X\cup X^{\prime}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma}\norm{[h_{X'}^{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau),\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)]}+\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{\Gamma\dv{\tau} \bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,X^{\prime}\subset\Lambda;\\
X\cup X^{\prime}\ni i\end{subarray}}\norm{[h_{X'}^{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau),\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)]}+V_{p}\Gamma f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})g(\bar{\Phi}_{q}).

(235)

The second term comes from the existence of
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
saturating Eq. (
234
).
In the first term, only the contributions from
X,X^{\prime}\in\Lambda
such that
X\cup X^{\prime}\ni i
and
X\cap X^{\prime}\neq\phi
survive.
Since
X
or
X^{\prime}
includes the site
i
, Eq. (
235
) is further bounded by

\displaystyle[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:sum_Phi_q_local})}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\left(\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{X^{\prime};X\cap X^{\prime}\neq\phi}+\sum_{X^{\prime}\ni i}\sum_{X;X\cap X^{\prime}\neq\phi}\right)\norm{[h_{X'}^{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau),\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)]}+V_{p}\Gamma f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})

(236)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\left(\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{j\in X}\sum_{X^{\prime}\ni j}+\sum_{X^{\prime}\ni i}\sum_{j\in X^{\prime}}\sum_{X\ni j}\right)2\norm{h_{X'}^{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau)}\norm{\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)}+V_{p}\Gamma f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle V_{p}(qk+k)2g\cdot g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})+V_{p}\Gamma f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})g(\bar{\Phi}_{q}).

In the last inequality, we use the fact that the domains of the local terms,
X
and
X^{\prime}
, respectively contain at most
qk
and
k
sites due to the locality.
We also use
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
that saturates Eq. (
234
).
Thus, one can choose the extensiveness
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q+1})
by

g(\bar{\Phi}_{q+1})=V_{p}\{2(q+1)kg+\Gamma f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})+\Gamma f\}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q}),

(237)

which is strictly larger than Eq. (
236
).
The quantity
\Gamma f
is added just for solving this recursive relation.

The strength of time-dependency is evaluated in a similar way.
Considering the Leibnitz rule, the time derivative of the local terms is bounded by

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\dv[q']{\tau}\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,X^{\prime}\subset\Lambda;\\
X\cup X^{\prime}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma}\norm{\dv[q']{\tau}[h_{X'}^{\gamma_{q+1}}(\tau),\bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)]}+\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{\Gamma\dv[q'+1]{\tau} \bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q}(\tau)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q+1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,X^{\prime}\subset\Lambda;\\
X\cup X^{\prime}\ni i\end{subarray}}\sum_{q^{\prime\prime}=0}^{q^{\prime}}\frac{q^{\prime}!}{q^{\prime\prime}!(q^{\prime}-q^{\prime\prime})!}\norm{\left[ h_{X'}^{\gamma_{q+1} (q'')}(\tau), \bar{h}_X^{\gamma_1,\cdots,\gamma_q (q'-q'')}(\tau)\right]}+V_{p}\Gamma[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})]^{q^{\prime}+1}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{q^{\prime\prime}=0}^{q^{\prime}}\frac{q^{\prime}!}{q^{\prime\prime}!(q^{\prime}-q^{\prime\prime})!}V_{p}(qk+k)2f^{q^{\prime\prime}}g\cdot[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})]^{q^{\prime}-q^{\prime\prime}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})+V_{p}\Gamma[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})]^{q^{\prime}+1}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})+f]^{q^{\prime}}V_{p}[2(q+1)kg+\Gamma f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})]g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})\leq[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})+f]^{q^{\prime}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q+1}).

(238)

Thus, one can choose the strength of time-dependency by

f(\bar{\Phi}_{q+1})=f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})+f.

(239)

We immediately obtain
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
with the initial condition
f(\bar{\Phi}_{1})=f
.
We substitute this into Eq. (
237
) and solve the resulting recursive relation
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q+1})=(q+1)V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
with
g(\bar{\Phi}_{1})=V_{p}g
.
This results in
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})=q!(V_{p})^{q}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q-1}g
.
\quad\square

C.2
Error by the truncated Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula

The next step for evaluating the error bound is to interleave the truncated BCH formula between the exact time-evolution operator and the time-dependent MPF.
Although the power of the time-independent PF
[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
in the MPF is not expressed in a simple way, that for the truncated BCH formula can be easily calculated as

[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}=\exp\left(-iH^{F}t-i\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}\frac{t^{q}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}\right).

(240)

Thus, the evaluation of the error
[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}-e^{-iH^{F}t}
is easier than that of
[\bar{T}_{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}-e^{-iH^{F}t}
included in Eq. (
165
).
We leave the calculation of the former one in Appendix
C.4
, and we begin with considering the error caused by adopting
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})
instead of
\bar{T}_{F}(t/k_{j})
in this section.

First, we focus on the error by replacing the PF for a single step, i.e.,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{F}(t)|0}-\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\bar{T}_{p_{0}(N,\epsilon)}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t)|0}.

(241)

We prove the following upper bound by using the locality of Hamiltonians.

Lemma C3
.

(Truncated BCH)

Let
\epsilon
be an arbitrary fixed value in
(0,1)
.
Suppose that the time
t
is small enough to satisfy

|t|\leq\frac{1}{4e^{3}V_{p}p_{0}(N,\epsilon)(2kg+\Gamma f)}\in\order{\frac{1}{(g+\Gamma f)\log(N/\epsilon)}},

(242)

where
p_{0}(N,\epsilon)=\lceil\log(2N/\epsilon)\rceil
denotes the truncation order.
The error of replacing the time-independent PF
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
by the truncated BCH formula
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t)
is bounded by

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\norm{\sum_{l \in\bbZ} e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\left[ \bar{T}^F(t)- \bar{T}_{p_0(N,\epsilon)}^\mr{BCH}(t)\right]|0}}\leq\epsilon.

(243)

Remark.—

We note that the truncation at the order
p_{0}(N,\epsilon)
plays an essential role in the MPF.
If we sets
p_{0}(N,\epsilon)\to\infty
, the error Eq. (
243
) becomes exactly zero, which seems to be preferable compared to the truncated case.
However, such a situation demands the convergence of the BCH formula Eq. (
220
).
According to its convergence radius, no bound tighter than
|t|\in\order{[N(g+\Gamma f)]^{-1}}
is known in general, like the time-independent case
[
43
]
.
It is incompatible with the complexity respecting the commutator scaling since we use the time-dependent MPF for the small time
t/r\in\tilde{\Omega}(N^{-1/(p+1)})
characterized by the Trotter number
r
in Eq. (
183
).
On the other hand, this lemma exploits the asymptotic convergence up to the order
p_{0}(N,\epsilon)
under the locality of Hamiltonians, allowing us to use the truncated BCH formula under the condition looser than the convergence radius, i.e., Eq. (
242
).
The time under Eq. (
242
) is compatible with the Trotter number by Eq. (
183
), whose system-size dependence reflects the locality via the commutator scaling.
The necessity of this truncation is present also in the time-independent MPF, and see Refs.
[
1
,
43
]
for its detailed discussion.

Proof.—

Let
p_{0}
denote
p_{0}(N,\epsilon)
for simplicity.
For the
N
-site lattice
\Lambda=\{1,2,\cdots,N\}
, we consider its subsystem,

\Lambda_{i}=\begin{cases}\Lambda&(i=0)\\
\{i+1,\cdots,N\}&(i=1,\cdots,N-1)\\
\phi&(i=N)\end{cases}.

(244)

Let us define the subsystem Hamiltonians,

H^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)=\sum_{X\subset\Lambda_{i}}h_{X}(t),\quad H^{\Lambda_{i}}_{\gamma}(t)=\sum_{X\subset\Lambda_{i}}h_{X}^{\gamma}(t).

(245)

The extensiveness and the strength of
H^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
, which are counterparts of Eqs. (
129
) and (
131
), are respectively smaller than those of
H(t)
by definition.
We consider the PFs and the BCH formula for the subsystem Hamiltonian
H^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
, for which we attach the superscript
\Lambda_{i}
.
For instance, the standard PF is defined by

\bar{S}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t,0)=\prod_{v=1}^{V_{p}}\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}e^{-iH_{\pi_{v}(\gamma)}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\beta_{v\gamma}t)\alpha_{v\gamma}t},

(246)

and each term in the BCH formula
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
is given in a similar manner to Eq. (
221
).
We also define the subsystem PF
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
and the subsystem BCH formula and the subsystem BCH formula
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)
as well.
The time-independent PF
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
and the BCH formula
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH}}_{p_{0}}(t)
are respectively decomposed into

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{F}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{iH^{\mathrm{LP}}t}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left[\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)\right],

(247)

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH}}_{p_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{iH^{\mathrm{LP}}t}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left[\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_{0}}(t)\right]

(248)

since we have
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{0}}(t)=\bar{T}^{F}(t)
,
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)=\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH}}_{p_{0}}(t)
, and
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{N}}(t)=\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{N}}_{p_{0}}(t)=e^{iH^{\mathrm{LP}}t}
.

We express the error in Eq. (
243
) by the above decompositions.
First, it is easy to check that the translation symmetry like Theorem
5
holds also for the operators
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
.
By calculation like Eq. (
88
), we obtain

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{F}(t)|0}=\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)|0}\right).

(249)

We note that the above relation is ensured by the absolute convergence of
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)|0}
, which is easily confirmed by Lemma
A3
and Lemma
A4
.
The translation symmetry of
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
also implies the unitarity of the operator in the above formula by

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)|0}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)|0}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum_{l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l-l^{\prime}}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)\ket{l}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)\ket{0}=I.

(250)

Similar calculation goes also for the subsystem BCH formula,
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t)
, leading to the decomposition like Eq. (
249
) and the unitarity like Eq. (
250
).
As a result, the left-hand side of the error, Eq. (
243
), is bounded by

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\norm{\sum_{l \in\bbZ} e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\left[ \bar{T}^F(t)- \bar{T}_{p_0}^\mr{BCH}(t)\right]|0}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\norm{\sum_{l \in\bbZ} \braket{l|\left[ \bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_i}(t)^\dagger\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t) - \bar{T}^{\mr{BCH},\Lambda_i}_{p_0}(t)^\dagger\bar{T}^{\mr{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_0}(t) \right]|0}}.

(251)

With a technique similar to the time-independent case
[
43
]
, we can expand the right-hand side with respect to
t
under the condition Eq. (
242
) as we will prove as Lemma
C5
and Lemma
C6
in Appendix
C.3
.
First, about the operator
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
, we can show the relation,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)\ket{0}=\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}t^{q}+\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t),\quad\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t)\in\order{t^{p_0+1}}.

(252)

Each of the operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
and
\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t)
is bounded by

\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^i}t^{q}\leq\frac{e}{2}[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q},\quad\norm{\bar{T}^{i}_{p_0}(t)}\leq e^{2}[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}.

(253)

As well, we have a similar expansion for the operator
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)
, given by

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_{0}}(t)\ket{0}=\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}t^{q}+\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t),

(254)

where the operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
and
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)
are respectively bounded by

\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}}t^{q}\leq\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\{4e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t\}^{q},\quad\norm{\bar{T}_{p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}(t)}\leq e^{3/2}[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}.

(255)

We attach their detailed derivation in Appendix
C.3
and continue the proof of Lemma
C3
.

Owing to the order condition
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)=\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)+\order{t^{p_0+1}}
, we have
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}=\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
for every
q=0,1,\cdots,p_{0}
.
Equation (
251
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\norm{\sum_{l \in\bbZ} e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\left[ \bar{T}^F(t)- \bar{T}_{p_0}^\mr{BCH}(t)\right]|0}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\sum_{q=p_{0}+1}^{\infty}\left(\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^i}t^{q}+\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}}t^{q}\right)+\norm{\bar{T}^i_{p_0}(t)}+\norm{\bar{T}_{p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}(t)}\right]

(256)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N\sum_{n=p_{0}+1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{e}{2}(2e^{3})^{-q}+\frac{1}{2e^{2}}e^{-q}\right]+e^{2}N(2e^{3})^{-p_{0}-1}+e^{3/2}N(2e)^{-p_{0}-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N\left(\frac{e+e^{-2}}{2}\sum_{q=p_{0}+1}^{\infty}e^{-q}+\frac{e^{-2}+e^{1/2}}{2}e^{-p_{0}}\right)\leq 2Ne^{-p_{0}}.

Since the integer
p_{0}=p_{0}(N,\epsilon)
is set to
\lceil\log(2N/\epsilon)\rceil
, this becomes smaller than
\epsilon
.
\quad\square

The aim of this section is to evaluate

\bar{N}(t,0)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|[T_{p_{0}(N,\epsilon)}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}|0}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\left\{[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}-[\bar{T}_{p_{0}(N,\epsilon)}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}\right\}|0},

(257)

which represents the error by replacing the repeated PF
[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
with the corresponding truncated BCH formula
[\bar{T}_{p_{0}(N,\epsilon)}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
.
It can be expressed by the single-step error discussed in Lemma
C3
, which results in the following statement.

Lemma C4
.

The standard MPF
\bar{N}(t,0)
is approximated as

\bar{N}(t,0)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\norm{\bar{N}(t,0)-\sum_{j=1}^J c_j \sum_{l \in\bbZ} e^{-il\omega t} \braket{l|[\bar{T}_{p_0(N,\epsilon)}^{\mr{BCH}} (t/k_j) ]^{k_j}|0}}\leq\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\|\vec{k}\|_{1}\epsilon,

(258)

under the same condition as Lemma
C3
.

Proof.—

The error of replacing
[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
in the time-dependent MPF
\bar{N}(t,0)
by the truncated BCH formula
[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
is equal to

\displaystyle\bar{N}(t,0)-\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\{\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})\}^{k_{j}}|0}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{k_{j}^{\prime}=1}^{k_{j}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\bra{l}[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}-k_{j}^{\prime}}\left(\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})-\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})\right)[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}^{\prime}-1}\ket{0}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{k_{j}^{\prime}=1}^{k_{j}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\bra{l}[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}-k_{j}^{\prime}}\ket{0}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\sum_{l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il^{\prime}\omega tk_{j}^{\prime}/k_{j}}\bra{l^{\prime}}\left[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})-\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})\right]\ket{0}\sum_{l^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il^{\prime\prime}\omega t(k_{j}^{\prime}-1)/k_{j}}\bra{l^{\prime\prime}}[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}^{\prime}-1}\ket{0},

(259)

where we use the completeness, the translation symmetry, and the absolute convergence like Eq. (
88
).
With calculation similar to Eq. (
250
), we can check that the two operators appearing in the above formula,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\bra{l}[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}-k_{j}^{\prime}}\ket{0},\qquad\sum_{l^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il^{\prime\prime}\omega t(k_{j}^{\prime}-1)/k_{j}}\bra{l^{\prime\prime}}[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}^{\prime}-1}\ket{0},

(260)

are both unitary.
As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{N}(t,0)-\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\norm{\bar{N}(t,0)-\sum_j c_j \sum_{l \in\bbZ}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|[ \bar{T}_{p_0}^\mr{BCH}(t/k_j) ]^{k_j}|0}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}|c_{j}|\sum_{k_{j}^{\prime}=1}^{k_{j}}\sum_{l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il^{\prime}\omega tk_{j}^{\prime}/k_{j}}\norm{\sum_{l' \in\bbZ} e^{-il'\omega tk_j'/k_j}\bra{l'}\left[\bar{T}^F(t/k_j)-\bar{T}_{p_0}^\mr{BCH}(t/k_j) \right] \ket{0}}\left[\bar{T}^{F}(t/k_{j})-\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})\right]\ket{0}

(261)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\|\vec{k}\|_{1}\epsilon,

where we use Lemma
C3
.
\quad\square

Considering the triangle inequality, the error of the time-dependent MPF is bounded by

\norm{U(t,0)-\bar{N}(t,0)}\leq U(t,0)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\norm{U(t,0)-\sum_{j=1}^J c_j \sum_{l \in\bbZ}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|[ \bar{T}_{p_0}^\mr{BCH}(t/k_j) ]^{k_j}|0}}+\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\|\vec{k}\|_{1}\epsilon.

(262)

We will evaluate the first term and complete the error bound in Appendix
C.4
.

C.3
Proof of the relations for the subsystem operators

In this section, we prove the relations Eqs. (
252
)-(
255
), provided by Lemma
C5
and Lemma
C6
.
First of all, we clarify the notations of some operators nontrivially acting on the

\Lambda_{i}
.
Using the subsystem Hamiltonian defined in Eq. (
245
), the time-independent subsystem PF on the Floquet-Hilbert space like Eq. (
49
) is precisely given by

\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)=\prod_{v=1}^{V_{p}}\prod_{\tilde{\gamma}=1}^{2\Gamma}e^{-i\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}}\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}t},\quad(\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}},\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}})=\begin{cases}(H_{\pi_{v}(\gamma)}^{\mathrm{Add},\Lambda_{i}}\alpha_{v\gamma})&(\tilde{\gamma}=2\gamma-1)\\
(-H^{\mathrm{LP}},\beta_{v(\gamma+1)}-\beta_{v\gamma})&(\tilde{\gamma}=2\gamma),\end{cases}

(263)

in which the operator
H_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{Add},\Lambda_{i}}
is defined by

H_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{Add},\Lambda_{i}}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{Add}_{m}\otimes H_{\gamma m}^{\Lambda_{i}},\quad H_{\gamma m}^{\Lambda_{i}}=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}H_{\gamma}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)e^{im\omega t}\differential t.

(264)

Similarly, the subsystem BCH formula
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\text{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}(t)
is precisely written by

\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\text{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}(t)=\exp\left(-iH^{F,\Lambda_{i}}t-i\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}t^{q}\right).

(265)

The Floquet Hamiltonian
H^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
is defined for the subsystem Hamiltonian
H^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
like Eq. (
30
) and the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
is obtained by replacing all of
\{\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}\}
in Eq. (
221
) with
\{\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}}\}
.
We first show the relations about the subsystem PF
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
, Eqs. (
252
) and (
253
), as follows.

Lemma C5
.

Let
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
denotes the subsystem PF on the Floquet-Hilbert space defined by Eq. (
263
).
When the time
t
is small enough to satisfy

t<\frac{1}{2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)}\in\order{[p_0(g+\Gamma f)]^{-1}}

(266)

for a certain natural number
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
, the operator
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)\ket{0}
is expanded by an absolutely convergent series,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)\ket{0}=\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}t^{q}+\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t).

(267)

The operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
and
\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t)
on the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
satisfy the following relations,

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^i}t^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e}{2}(4p_{0}V_{p}kgt+2p_{0}V_{p}\Gamma ft)^{q}\in\order{[p_0(g+\Gamma f)t]^p},

(268)

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}^{i}_{p_0}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{2}(4p_{0}V_{p}kgt+2p_{0}V_{p}\Gamma ft)^{p_{0}+1}\in\order{[p_0(g+\Gamma f)t]^{p_0+1}}.

(269)

Proof.—

Since
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
is a time-dependent PF defined by Eq. (
49
), we can apply the calculation for the time-independent case to the coefficient
\bar{T}^{F,i}_{q}
(See Lemma 9 in Ref.
[
43
]
).
We define two kinds of time-dependent Hamiltonians
h_{i}^{F}(\tau)
and
\tilde{H}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)
for the time
\tau\in[0,2V_{p}\Gamma t)
, which act on the Floquet-Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{FH}}
.
They are piecewise constant and defined by

h_{i}^{F}(\tau)=\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}-\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}}),\quad\tilde{H}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)=\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}},\quad\frac{\tau}{t}\in[(v-1)2\Gamma+\tilde{\gamma}-1,(v-1)2\Gamma+\tilde{\gamma}),

(270)

for each
v=1,\cdots,V_{p}
and each
\tilde{\gamma}=1,\cdots,2\Gamma
.
The operator
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)
defined by Eq. (
263
) is equal to the time-evolution operator under the Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)
over the time
\tau\in[0,2V_{p}\Gamma t]
.
Considering the interaction picture in the Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)
, it can be also represented by

\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(-i\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma t}\differential\tau U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(\tau)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)\right),\quad U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)=\mathcal{T}\exp\left(-i\int_{0}^{\tau}\differential\tau^{\prime}\tilde{H}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau^{\prime})\right).

(271)

Deriving the series expansion, Eq. (
267
).—

We first derive the coefficient of the series expansion
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
.
Applying the Dyson series expansion to the operator
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)\ket{0}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{q^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-i)^{q^{\prime}}}{q^{\prime}!}\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma t}\differential\tau_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma t}\differential\tau_{q^{\prime}}\bra{l}\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{q^{\prime\prime}=1}^{q^{\prime}}U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}})^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}})U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}})\right]\ket{0},

(272)

where the symbol
\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}
denotes the time-ordered product, in which
\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{q^{\prime}}
are aligned in the descending order.
Owing to the smoothness of the Hamiltonian discussed in Appendix
A
, we have at least
\norm{\braket{l|U^{F,\Lambda_i}(\tau)|0}}\in\order{|l|^{-2}}
and
\norm{\braket{l|h_i^F(\tau)|0}}\in\order{|l|^{-2}}
for
|\tau|<\infty
.
Using Lemma
A3
, there exists a certain positive constant
C_{2}
such that

\frac{(-i)^{q^{\prime}}}{q^{\prime}!}\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma t}\differential\tau_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma t}\differential\tau_{q^{\prime}}\norm{\frac{(-i)^{q'}}{q'!} \int_0^{2V_p\Gamma t} \dd\tau_1 \cdots\int_0^{2V_p\Gamma t} \dd\tau_{q'} \bra{l} \mcl{T}_\rightarrow\left[ \prod_{q''=1}^{q'} U^{F,\Lambda_i}(\tau_{q''})^\dagger h_i^F(\tau_{q''}) U^{F,\Lambda_i}(\tau_{q''})\right] \ket{0}}\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{q^{\prime\prime}=1}^{q^{\prime}}U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}})^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}})U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}})\right]\ket{0}\leq\frac{(2V_{p}\Gamma t)^{q^{\prime}}(C_{2})^{q^{\prime}}}{q^{\prime}!(1+|l|)^{2}}.

(273)

Thus, the infinite series by Eq. (
272
) is absolutely convergent with respect to
l
and
q^{\prime}
, and we can change the order of taking the summation freely.
As a result, in a similar manner to the calculation for Eq. (
88
), we obtain

[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:TT_sum_Dyson})}]=\sum_{q^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-it)^{q^{\prime}}}{q^{\prime}!}\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s_{q^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{q^{\prime\prime}=1}^{q^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(s_{q^{\prime\prime}}t)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(s_{q^{\prime\prime}}t)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(s_{q^{\prime\prime}}t)\ket{0}\right)\right].

(274)

We use the translation symmetry
\braket{l|U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(\tau)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)|l^{\prime}}=\braket{l-l^{\prime\prime}|U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(\tau)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)|l^{\prime}-l^{\prime\prime}}
, which is easily obtained by Eqs. (
34
)-(
36
), and change the variables in the integral by
\tau_{q^{\prime\prime}}=s_{q^{\prime\prime}}t
in the above.

We next consider the expansion of the operator
U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(st)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(st)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(st)
in the integral.
Let
v(s)
and
\tilde{\gamma}(s)
denote the indices such that
s\in[(v(s)-1)2\Gamma+\tilde{\gamma}(s)-1,(v(s)-1)2\Gamma+\tilde{\gamma}(s))
.
It can be expressed by

U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(st)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(st)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(st)=\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{11}e^{i\tilde{\alpha}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{s}t\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}}}\right)h_{i}^{F}(st),\quad\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{s}=\begin{cases}\tilde{\alpha}_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}(s-\lfloor s\rfloor)&\text{if $v\tilde{\gamma}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)$}\\
\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

(275)

and the coefficient
\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{s}
is bounded by
|\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{s}|\leq|\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}|\leq 1
.
We can employ Eq. (
84
) with the replacement of the order
p
by
p_{0}
, which is the result of the Taylor’s theorem.
This leads to the series expansion,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(st)^{\dagger}h_{i}^{F}(st)U^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(st)\ket{0}=\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)t^{q}+\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s),

(276)

where the operators
\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)
and
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
are respectively defined by

\displaystyle\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i^{q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{11},\cdots,q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\geq 0,\\
q_{11}+\cdots+q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}=q\end{subarray}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{11}\frac{(\tilde{\alpha}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{s}\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}})^{q_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}}}{q_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}!}\right)h_{i}^{F}(st)\ket{0},

(277)

\displaystyle\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(it)^{p_{0}+1}\sum_{v\tilde{\gamma}\preceq v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{v\tilde{\gamma}},\cdots,q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\geq 0,\\
q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}\neq 0,\\
q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}+\cdots+q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}=p_{0}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{1}\differential u\frac{u^{q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}-1}}{(q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}-1)!q_{v(\tilde{\gamma}+1)}!\cdots q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}!}

(278)

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(\tilde{\gamma}+1)}^{11}e^{i\tilde{\alpha}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{s}t\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}}}\right)e^{i\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{s}ut\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}}}}\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tilde{\alpha}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{s}\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}})^{q_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}}\right)h_{i}^{F}(st)\ket{0}.

The operator
\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)
is independent of the time
t
since
h_{i}^{F}(\tau)
is determined by the time
\tau
modulo
t
according to Eq. (
270
).
We note that the above calculation executes the reordering of the infinite summation with respect to
l\in\mathbb{Z}
.
It is justified by the absolute convergence of the operators
\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)
and
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
with respect to
l\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We use the assumption
h_{X}^{\gamma}(t)\in C^{\infty}
for this purpose.
Since the Fourier coefficients like
\braket{l|H_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{Add}}|l^{\prime}}
and
\braket{l|h_{i}^{F}(st)|l^{\prime}}
decay faster than every polynomial of
l
as discussed at the end of Appendix
A
, each term in the summation appearing in Eq. (
277
) rapidly decay at least as
o(|l|^{-2})
.
This implies the absolute convergence of
\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)
and so does also for
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
.
If we only assume
h_{X}^{\gamma}(t)\in C^{p+2}
like the time-dependent PF, the above series may not be absolutely convergent for
q=p+1,\cdots,p_{0}
, which involve differentiation more than
p
times.
The reason why we employ the Taylor’s theorem in Eq. (
276
) rather than the infinite series expansion in contrast to Eq. (
272
) is the absolute convergence, too.
Since
\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}}
contains
H^{\mathrm{LP}}
, which is an unbounded operator, each term in Eq. (
277
) can have a norm whose scaling is
\order{|l|^q}
.
We cannot ensure the absolute convergence of Eq. (
276
) if
q
can be infinite, which leads to the truncation at the order
p_{0}
.

We substitute the expansion Eq. (
276
) into Eq. (
272
) and rearrange the terms by the order in time
t
.
The coefficient
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
comes from the collection of the products of
\{\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)\}
and it is given by

\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}=\sum_{n=0}^{q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q-n\end{subarray}}\frac{(-i)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s_{n}\,\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Xi_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{i}(s_{n^{\prime}})\right].

(279)

The symbol
\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}
means the time-ordered product that aligns
s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}
in the descending order.
The operator
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t)
comes from the remaining terms involving the Taylor’s reminder
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
, and it is given by

\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-it)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s_{n}\,\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}\Xi_{q}^{i}(s_{n^{\prime}})t^{q}+\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s_{n^{\prime}})\right)-\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}\Xi_{q}^{i}(s_{n^{\prime}})t^{q}\right)\right].

(280)

Upper-bounding the coefficients.—

We next derive the upper bounds on
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
and
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t)
respectively defined by Eqs. (
279
) and (
280
).
We begin with evaluating
\Xi_{q}^{i}(s)
and
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
in them, which are respectively defined by Eqs. (
277
) and (
278
).
When the integer
\tilde{\gamma}(s)
is even, we have
\norm{\Xi_q^i(s)}=0
due to
h_{i}^{F}(st)=0
by Eqs. (
74
) and (
270
).
When the integer
\tilde{\gamma}(s)
is odd with
\tilde{\gamma}(s)=2\gamma(s)-1
, it is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\Xi_q^i(s)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{11},\cdots,q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\geq 0,\\
q_{11}+\cdots+q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}=q\end{subarray}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\norm{\sum_{l \in\bbZ} \bra{l} \left(\prod_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{11} \frac{\left( \ad_{\tilde{H}_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}^{\Lambda_i}}\right)^{q_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}}}{q_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}!} \right) h_i^F(st)\ket{0}}\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{11}\frac{\left(\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}}\right)^{q_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}}}{q_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}!}\right)h_{i}^{F}(st)\ket{0}

(281)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{11},\cdots,q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\geq 0,\\
q_{11}+\cdots+q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}=q\end{subarray}}\norm{ \left(\prod_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{11} \frac{[ \bar{D}_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}^{\Lambda_i}(0)]^{q_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}}}{q_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}!} \right) (H_{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(0)-H_{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}^{\Lambda_{i}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\sum_{X\subset\Lambda;X\ni i}\norm{\left( \prod_{q'=1}^q \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_{q'}}^{\Lambda_i} (0) \right) h_X^{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}(0)},

where the operators
\bar{D}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
and
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
are defined by replacing
H_{\gamma}(t)
with
H_{\gamma}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
respectively in Eq. (
91
) and (
45
).
In the above calculation, the second line comes from the fact that the nested commutators among
\{H_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{Add}}\}
and
H^{\mathrm{LP}}
are converted to those among
\{H_{\gamma}(t)\}
and their time derivatives, as discussed in Lemma
9
.
In the last inequality, we use the fact that the summation over
q_{11},\cdots,q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}
such that
q_{11}+\cdots+q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}=q
is covered by
(V_{p})^{q}
copies of the summation over
\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}
, as discussed in Eqs. (
113
)-(
121
).
We also take into account that

H_{\gamma}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)-H_{\gamma}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)=\sum_{X\subset\Lambda_{i};X\ni i}h_{X}^{\gamma}

(282)

is covered by all the terms
\{h_{X}^{\gamma}(t)\}
nontrivially acting on the site
i
.
When we integrate
\norm{\Xi_q(s)}
over
s\in(0,2V_{p}\Gamma)
, the index
\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))
reproduces every index
\gamma
for
V_{p}
times, and hence we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s\norm{\Xi_q(s)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q+1}\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\sum_{X\subset\Lambda;X\ni i}\norm{\left( \prod_{q'=1}^q \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_{q'}}^{\Lambda_i} (0) \right) h_X^\gamma(0)}

(283)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q+1}q!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q}g

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[qV_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{q+1}.

The second inequality comes from Theorem
14
, which is valid also for the subsystem Hamiltonian
H^{\mathrm{\Lambda_{i}}}(t)
since it shares the locality, the extensiveness by Eq. (
129
), and the scale of time-dependency by Eq. (
131
) with
H(t)
.

We next evaluate the upper bound of the operator
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
given by Eq. (
278
).
We trivially have
\norm{\Xi_{p_0}^i(t;s)}=0
when
\tilde{\gamma}(s)
is even.
When
\tilde{\gamma}(s)
is odd by
\tilde{\gamma}(s)=2\gamma(s)-1
, the calculation similar to Lemma
10
ensures the existence of a certain time
\tau_{s,u}\in[0,t]
and a unitary operator
\bar{U}_{v\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}(\tau_{s,u})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(\tilde{\gamma}+1)}^{11}e^{i\tilde{\alpha}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{s}t\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}}}\right)e^{i\tilde{\alpha}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{s}ut\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v\tilde{\gamma}}^{\Lambda_{i}}}}\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tilde{\alpha}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{s}\mathrm{ad}_{\tilde{H}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}})^{q_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}}\right)h_{i}^{F}(st)\ket{0}

\displaystyle\quad=\bar{U}_{v\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}(\tau_{s,u})^{\dagger}\left[\left(\prod_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{v\tilde{\gamma}}\bar{D}_{v^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}(\tau_{s,u})\right)\alpha_{v(s)\gamma(s)}[H_{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau_{s,u})-H_{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau_{s,u})]\right]\bar{U}_{v\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}(\tau_{s,u}).

(284)

The norm of the operator
\Xi_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\Xi_{p_0}^i(t;s)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle t^{p_{0}+1}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left(\sum_{v\tilde{\gamma}\preceq v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{v\tilde{\gamma}},\cdots,q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}\geq 0,\\
q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}+\cdots+q_{v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}=p_{0}+1,\\
q_{v\tilde{\gamma}}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\sum_{X\subset\Lambda;X\ni i}\norm{\left(\prod_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'=v(s)\tilde{\gamma}(s)}^{v\tilde{\gamma}} \bar{D}_{v'\tilde{\gamma}'}(\tau) \right)h_X^{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}(\tau)}\right)

(285)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p}t)^{p_{0}+1}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left(\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{p_{0}+1}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\sum_{X\subset\Lambda;X\ni i}\norm{\left( \prod_{q'=1}^{p_0+1} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_{q'}} (\tau) \right) h_X^{\pi_{v(s)}(\gamma(s))}(\tau)}\right),

where the second inequality is obtained in parallel to Eq. (
281
).
Its integral is bounded by

\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s\norm{\Xi_{p_0}^i(t;s)}\leq t^{p_{0}+1}[(p_{0}+1)V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{p_{0}+2}.

(286)

as well as Eq. (
283
).

We are ready to prove the upper bounds on the operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
and
\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t)
.
Using the expression of
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{i}
by Eq. (
279
), its norm is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q-n\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n!}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s\norm{\Xi_{q_{n'}}^i(s)}\right)

(287)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q-n\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n!}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[q_{n^{\prime}}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)\right]^{q_{n^{\prime}}+1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{q-1}[p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{q}\sum_{n=0}^{q}\frac{1}{n!}\leq\frac{e}{2}[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{q}.

We use Eq. (
283
) in the second inequality and use
q_{n^{\prime}}\leq p_{0}
in the third inequality.
This completes the proof of Eq. (
268
).
We note that this bound also implies the absolute convergence of the series Eq. (
267
) with respect to
q
under the condition Eq. (
266
).
On the other hand, the norm of the operator
\bar{T}^{i}_{p_{0}}(t)
expressed by Eq. (
280
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}^i_{p_0}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{n!}{n^{\prime}!(n-n^{\prime})!}\left(\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s\norm{\Xi_{p_0}^i(t;s)}\right)^{n^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}\int_{0}^{2V_{p}\Gamma}\differential s\norm{\Xi_q^i(s)}t^{q}\right)^{n-n^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{n!}{n^{\prime}!(n-n^{\prime})!}\left\{t^{p_{0}+1}[(p_{0}+1)V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{p_{0}+2}\right\}^{n^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}t^{q}[qV_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{q+1}\right)^{n-n^{\prime}}.

The condition on the time
t
by Eq. (
266
) implies the relation,

\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}[qV_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}\leq\sum_{q=0}^{p_{0}}[p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}\\
\leq\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}2^{-q}=2.

As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}^i_{p_0}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{n}}{n!}\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{n!}{n^{\prime}!(n-n^{\prime})!}\left\{[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}\right\}^{n^{\prime}}

(289)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{2^{n}}{n!}[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}=e^{2}[2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1},

which completes the proof of Eq. (
269
).
\quad\square

We briefly explain the meaning of Lemma
C5
.
The subsystem PFs
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
and
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)
are global operators on
\Lambda_{i}
and
\Lambda_{i-1}
, whose sizes can be as large as the system size
N
.
On the other hand, the operators
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)
and
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)
have different actions only around the site
i
by construction, and hence
\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}(t)
becomes quasi-local around the site
i
.
This is why the upper bounds of
\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^i}
and
\norm{\bar{T}^i_{p_0}(t)}
, appearing in its series expansion, are determined by the local scale of the energy
g
and that of time-dependency
f
without explicitly depending on the size
N
except for logarithmic corrections by
p_{0}(N,\epsilon)
.
This property originates from the locality of Hamiltonians, and hence it lies also in the subsystem BCH formula
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)
.
As a counterpart of Lemma
C5
, we prove the following lemma corresponding to Eq. (
255
) in Appendix
C.2
.

Lemma C6
.

Let
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)
denote the subsystem BCH formula on the Floquet-Hilbert space defined by Eq. (
265
) and suppose that the time
t
is small enough to satisfy

|t|<\frac{1}{4e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)}\in\order{[p_0(g+\Gamma f)]^{-1}},

(290)

for a certain natural number
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
. The operator
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_{0}}(t)|0}
is expanded by an absolutely convergent series,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_{0}}(t)\ket{0}=\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}t^{q}+\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t).

(291)

where the operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
and
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)
are bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}}t^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2e^{2}}[4e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}\in\order{[p_0(g+\Gamma f)t]^q},

(292)

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}_{p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{3/2}[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}\in\order{[p_0(g+\Gamma f)t]^{p_0+1}}.

(293)

Proof.—

The proof is parallel to the one for Lemma
C5
.
Namely, we first execute the series expansion in the time
t
and obtain the expressions of the operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
and
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)
.
Then, we evaluate the upper bounds of them using the locality, the extensiveness, and the strength of time-dependency discussed in Appendix
C.1
.
In the calculation below, we need to ensure the absolute convergence of the infinite series.
However, it is essentially the same as the discussion in Lemma
C5
in that the assumption on the smoothness is sufficient to ensure it, and hence we omit the discussion for the absolute convergence.

Deriving the series expansion, Eq. (
291
).—

The operator
\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_{0}}(t)
can be recast by a time-evolution operator under the interaction picture like Eq. (
271
).
Regarding the operators
\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}t^{q-1}
and
\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q-1}
respectively as an unperturbed Hamiltonian and an interaction Hamiltonian, we obtain the relation,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i}}_{p_{0}}(t)^{\dagger}\bar{T}^{\mathrm{BCH},\Lambda_{i-1}}_{p_{0}}(t)\ket{0}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-i)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{t}\differential\tau_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{t}\differential\tau_{n}\bra{l}\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{i\tau_{n^{\prime}}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q-1}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q-1}\right)\right]\ket{0}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-i)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}e^{is_{n^{\prime}}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q}\ket{0}\right)\right].

(294)

The second equality comes from the symmetry of the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
, which is equal to
H^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
for
q=1
and is zero or composed of nested commutators on the Floquet-Hilbert space for
q\geq 2
.
We have the translation symmetry,

\braket{l-l^{\prime\prime}|\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}|l^{\prime}-l^{\prime\prime}}=\braket{l|\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}|l^{\prime}}+l^{\prime\prime}\omega\delta_{q1},

(295)

by Lemma
9
, and the second equality follows from the calculation similar to Eq. (
88
).

We next expand the operators appearing in Eq. (
294
).
Using the Taylor’s theorem on an operator exponential by Eq. (
83
) up to the order
p_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}e^{is\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q}\ket{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{p_{0}}\frac{\left(is\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}\right)^{n}}{n!}+\int_{0}^{s}\differential s^{\prime}e^{is^{\prime}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}}\frac{\left(is^{\prime}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}\right)^{p_{0}+1}}{(p_{0}+1)!}\right]\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q}\ket{0}.

The above series can be arranged in terms of the time
t
and expressed by

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}e^{is\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q}\ket{0}=\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)t^{q}+\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s),

(297)

where we define the operators
\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)
and
\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
respectively by

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{p_{0}}\frac{(is)^{n}}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{0},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{0}+\cdots+q_{n}=q\end{subarray}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}\left(\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}\right)\left(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{0}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q_{0}}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}\right)\ket{0},

(298)

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\differential s^{\prime}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\bra{l}e^{is^{\prime}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}}\frac{\left(is^{\prime}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}\right)^{p_{0}+1}}{(p_{0}+1)!}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q}\ket{0}.

(299)

We note that
\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)
is equal to zero for
q>(p_{0})^{2}+p_{0}
.
We substitute the series expansion Eq. (
297
) into Eq. (
294
) and again arrange it in terms of the time
t
like Eqs. (
279
) and (
280
).
Consequently, we obtain the series expansion in the form of Eq. (
291
), in which the operators
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
and
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)
are respectively given by

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-i)^{n}}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\geq 1,\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\bar{\Psi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{i}(s_{n^{\prime}})\right],

(300)

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-i)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\mathcal{T}_{\rightarrow}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s_{n^{\prime}})t^{q}+\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s_{n^{\prime}})\right)-\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s_{n^{\prime}})t^{q}\right)\right].

Upper-bounding the norm of
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
.—
We next consider the upper bound on the norm of the operator
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
.
Focusing on the operator
\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)
given by Eq. (
298
), the commutator appearing in it is expressed by

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}})|0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\braket{l|\bar{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}|l^{\prime}}\braket{l^{\prime}|(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}})|0}-\braket{l|(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}})|l^{\prime}}\braket{l^{\prime}|\bar{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}|0}\right)

(302)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[\braket{l|\bar{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}|0},\braket{l^{\prime}|(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}})|0}\right]-\delta_{q1}\sum_{l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}l^{\prime}\omega\braket{l^{\prime}|(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}})|0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(0),\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{\Lambda_{i}}(0)-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(0)\right]+\delta_{q1}\left(i\derivative{t}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{\Lambda_{i}}(0)-i\derivative{t}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(0)\right).

We use the translation symmetry of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
by Eq. (
295
) for the second equality, and use the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{\Lambda_{i}}(t)
defined by Eq. (
223
) for the last equality.
Since the commutator
[\bar{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}^{F,\Lambda_{i}},\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}]
has the translation invariance like
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}
, the above calculation can be repeated for the nested commutators in Eq. (
298
).
We obtain the relation,

\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)=\sum_{n=0}^{p_{0}}\frac{(is)^{n}}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{0},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{0}+\cdots+q_{n}=q\end{subarray}}\left.\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)\left[\bar{\Phi}_{q_{0}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)-\bar{\Phi}_{q_{0}}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right]\right|_{\tau=0},\quad\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)=\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)}+\delta_{q_{n^{\prime}}1}i\derivative{\tau}\right),

(303)

for the operator
\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)
.

We consider the norm of each operator appearing in the summation, Eq. (
303
).
As discussed in Appendix
C.1
, every term of the BCH expansion
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)
is reproduced by
(V_{p})^{q}
copies of
\prod_{q^{\prime}=2}^{q}\bar{D}_{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)H_{\gamma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)
.
Each term with certain indices
\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{q}
for the subsystem
\Lambda_{i}
shares the same coefficient with the one with the same indices for the subsystem
\Lambda_{i-1}
by definition.
As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left[ \bar{\Phi}_{q_0}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)- \bar{\Phi}_{q_0}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau) \right]}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(V_{p})^{q_{0}}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left[ \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau) - \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right) H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau) \right]}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(V_{p})^{q_{0}}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) \left[ H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau) - H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right] }

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{q^{\prime}=2}^{q_{0}}(V_{p})^{q_{0}}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=q'+1}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) \left[ \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_{q'}}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau) - \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_{q'}}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right] \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q'-1} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right)H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau) }.

(304)

The last inequality comes from the triangular inequality.
As discussed in Eq. (
282
), all the local terms of
H_{\gamma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)-H_{\gamma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)
are covered by the set of
\{h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)\}
such that the domain
X
includes the site
i
.
Similarly, the operator
\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)-\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)
is composed of
\mathrm{ad}_{h_{X}^{\gamma_{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)}
with
X\ni i
for
\gamma_{q^{\prime}}=1,\cdots,\Gamma
, while it vanishes for
\gamma_{q^{\prime}}=\Gamma+1
.
Thus, Eq. (
304
) is further bounded by

\displaystyle[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:An_Phi_q_diff})}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q_{0}}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) h_X^{\gamma_1}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{q^{\prime}=2}^{q_{0}}(V_{p})^{q_{0}}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=q'+1}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) \left[ h_X^{\gamma_{q'}}(\tau) , \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q'-1} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right)H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right]}.

Using the locality, the extensiveness, and the strength of time-dependency of the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(\tau)
by Lemma
C2
, we can obtain the following relations,

\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) h_X^{\gamma_1}(\tau)}\leq\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[(2kg(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}})+f)\sum_{n^{\prime\prime}=0}^{n^{\prime}-1}q_{n^{\prime\prime}}\right](q_{0}-1)!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g,

(306)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=q'+1}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) \left[ h_X^{\gamma_{q'}}(\tau) , \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q'-1} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right)H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[(2kg(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}})+f)\sum_{n^{\prime\prime}=0}^{n^{\prime}-1}q_{n^{\prime\prime}}\right](q_{0}-1)!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g.

(307)

The quantity
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q})
denotes the extensiveness of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(\tau)
given by Eq. (
230
).
Since their derivation is too long and essentially parallel to the calculation in Section
VI.2
, we attach it at the end of this section and continue the proof of Lemma
C6
here.

Using the inequalities Eqs. (
306
) and (
307
), Eq. (
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left[ \bar{\Phi}_{q_0}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)- \bar{\Phi}_{q_0}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau) \right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q_{0}}[1+(q_{0}-1)]\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[(2kg(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}})+f)\sum_{n^{\prime\prime}=0}^{n^{\prime}-1}q_{n^{\prime\prime}}\right](q_{0}-1)!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g

(308)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(V_{p})^{q_{0}}q_{0}!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left\{\left[q_{n^{\prime}}!(V_{p})^{q_{n^{\prime}}}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{n^{\prime}}-1}2kg+f\right]q\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(p_{0}V_{p})^{q}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q-1}g(2q)^{n}.

In the last inequality, we consider the indices
q_{0},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}
such that
q_{0},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0}
and
q_{0}+\cdots+q_{n}=q
, which are valid relations in the summation Eq. (
303
), and employ the resulting inequality
q_{0}!\cdots q_{n}!\leq(p_{0})^{q_{0}+\cdots+q_{n}}=(p_{0})^{q}
.
The operator
\bar{\Psi}_{q}^{i}(s)
expressed by Eq. (
298
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{\Psi}_q^i(s)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{p_{0}}\frac{s^{n}}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{0},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{0}+\cdots+q_{n}=q\end{subarray}}(p_{0}V_{p})^{q}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q-1}g(2q)^{n}

(309)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\{2p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)\}^{q}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2sq)^{n}}{n!}=\frac{1}{2}\{2e^{2s}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)\}^{q}.

Finally, substituting this relation into Eq. (
300
), we arrive at the upper bound of the operator
\bar{T}_{q,p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}
give by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}_{q,p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}}t^{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0},\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{1}\cdots\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left\{\frac{1}{2}[2e^{2s_{n^{\prime}}}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)]^{q_{n^{\prime}}}\right\}t^{q}

(310)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{2^{q-n-1}}{n!}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}[4e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}.

This completes the proof of Eq. (
292
).

Upper-bounding the norm of
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)
.—

We consider the norm of the higher order term
\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH},i}(t)
.
Using Eq. (
), it is bounded by

\norm{\bar{T}_{p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}(t)}\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{n!}{n^{\prime}!(n-n^{\prime})!}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\differential s\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\norm{\bar{\Psi}_q^i(s)}t^{q}\right)^{n-n^{\prime}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\differential s\norm{\bar{\Psi}_{p_0}^i (t;s)}\right)^{n^{\prime}}.

(311)

The inequality Eq. (
309
) and the assumption Eq. (
290
) imply an upper bound,

\int_{0}^{1}\differential s\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\norm{\bar{\Psi}_q^i(s)}t^{q}\leq\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}\leq\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}2^{-n-1}=\frac{1}{2},

(312)

and hence it is sufficient to evaluate the upper bound on
\norm{\bar{\Psi}_{p_0}^i (t;s)}
.

Let us define an operator
\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(st)
by

\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(st)=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega st}\braket{l|e^{-is\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}t^{q}}|0}.

(313)

Then, using the symmetry of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}
by Eq. (
295
), the operator
\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(st)
is a unitary operator like Eq. (
250
) and gives an expression of
\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
by

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\differential s^{\prime}\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(s^{\prime}t)^{\dagger}\left[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega s^{\prime}t}\bra{l}\frac{\left(is^{\prime}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}t^{q}\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}}}\right)^{p_{0}+1}}{(p_{0}+1)!}\sum_{q=1}^{p_{0}}(\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i-1}}-\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F,\Lambda_{i}})t^{q}\ket{0}\right]\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(s^{\prime}t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\differential s^{\prime}\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(s^{\prime}t)^{\dagger}\left[\frac{(is^{\prime})^{p_{0}+1}}{(p_{0}+1)!}\sum_{q=p_{0}+1}^{p_{0}(p_{0}+2)}t^{q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{0},\cdots,q_{p_{0}+1}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{0}+\cdots+q_{p_{0}+1}=q\end{subarray}}\left.\mathcal{A}_{p_{0}+1}(\tau)\left(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{0}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)-\bar{\Phi}_{q_{0}}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right)\right|_{\tau=s^{\prime}t}\right]\bar{U}_{p_{0}}^{\bar{\Phi}}(s^{\prime}t).

The first equality comes from the calculation similar to Eq. (
88
), in which we use the translation symmetry Eq. (
295
).
The second equality is obtained in a similar manner to Eqs. (
302
) and (
303
) while the time
\tau=0
is replaced by
\tau=s^{\prime}t\in[0,t]
due to the phase factor
e^{-il\omega s^{\prime}t}
in the first line.
Employing the upper bound Eq. (
308
) under
n=p_{0}+1
, the operator
\bar{\Psi}_{p_{0}}^{i}(t;s)
has a norm bounded by

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{\Psi}_{p_0}^i (t;s)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{s^{p_{0}+1}}{(p_{0}+1)!}\sum_{q=p_{0}+1}^{\infty}t^{q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{0},\cdots,q_{p_{0}+1}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{0}+\cdots+q_{p_{0}+1}=q\end{subarray}}(p_{0}V_{p})^{q}(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q-1}g(2q)^{p_{0}+1}

(315)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}s^{p_{0}+1}\sum_{q=p_{0}+1}^{\infty}[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{q}\leq[2e^{2}sp_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}.

We use the relation
(2q)^{p_{0}+1}/(p_{0}+1)!\leq e^{2q}
and the assumption Eq. (
290
) respectively for the second and last inequalities.
Summarizing the relations, Eqs. (
311
), (
312
), and (
315
), we finally obtain

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{T}_{p_0}^{\mr{BCH},i}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{n!}{n^{\prime}!(n-n^{\prime})!}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{n-n^{\prime}}[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1}

(316)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{3/2}[2e^{2}p_{0}V_{p}(2kg+\Gamma f)t]^{p_{0}+1},

which completes the proof of Eq. (
293
).
\quad\square

Proof of Eq. (
306
).—

We first consider an upper bound of

\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{ \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) h_X^{\gamma_1}(\tau)},

(317)

which corresponds to the case,
n=0
.
In a similar manner to the discussion in Section
VI.2
, the operator
\prod_{q=2}^{q_{0}}\{\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)\}h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)
is expanded by

\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r}\subset\Lambda_{i}}[h_{X_{r}}^{\gamma_{r}(n_{r})}(\tau),\cdots,[h_{X_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}(n_{2})}(\tau),h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}(n_{1})}(\tau)]],

(318)

such that
r+n_{1}+\cdots+n_{r}=q_{0}-1
.
Its summation has a norm bounded by

\displaystyle\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}\sum_{\gamma_{1}}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r}\subset\Lambda_{i}}\norm{[h_{X_r}^{\gamma_r (n_r)}(\tau), \cdots, [h_{X_2}^{\gamma_2 (n_2)}(\tau),h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}(\tau)]]}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{r-1}\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\gamma_1(n_1)}(\tau)}\prod_{r^{\prime}=r}^{2}\left(\sum_{\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{r^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i};\\
X_{r^{\prime}}\cap Y_{r^{\prime}}\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\norm{h_{X_{r'}}^{\gamma_{r'}(n_{r'})}(\tau)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{r-1}\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}f^{n_{1}}g\prod_{r^{\prime}=2}^{r}\left[(r^{\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime}}}g\right]=(r-1)!(2kg)^{r-1}(\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1-r}g.

(319)

As discussed in Section
VI.2
, Eq. (
317
) is bounded by at most
(q_{0}-1)!/\{r!(q_{0}-r-1)!\}\times r^{q_{0}-1-r}
copies of Eq. (
318
).
The former factor comes from the number of ways to pick up
q_{0}-1-r
indices giving the time derivative
\mathcal{D}_{\Gamma+1}(\tau)=\Gamma\derivative{\tau}
.
The latter one means the maximal number of copies when the Leibnitz rule is taken into account.
As a result, Eq. (
317
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) h_X^{\gamma_1}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{q_{0}-1}\frac{(q_{0}-1)!}{r!(q_{0}-1-r)!}r^{q_{0}-1-r}(r-1)!(2kg)^{r-1}(\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-r-1}g

(320)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(q_{0}-1)!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g.

We next consider the case where the operator
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)
is applied.
The operator
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)\prod_{q=2}^{q_{0}}[\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)]h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)
is composed of nested commutators in the form of

\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r+r^{\prime}}\in\Lambda_{i}}[h_{X_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+r^{\prime}})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_{r+1}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+1})}(\tau),[h_{X_{r}}^{\gamma_{r}(n_{r})}(\tau),\cdots,[h_{X_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}(n_{2})}(\tau),h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}(n_{1})}(\tau)]]]]]

(321)

with some set of the integers
n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}
.
The operator
h_{X}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q}(n^{\prime})}(\tau)
denotes the
n^{\prime}
-th derivative of
h_{X}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q}}(\tau)
, which arises from the time derivative in
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)
.
The number
r^{\prime}
satisfies
r^{\prime}\leq n
since the operator
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)
applies at most
n
-fold commutators to Eq. (
318
).
The summation of Eq. (
321
) over
X\ni i
and
\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}
has a norm bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r+r^{\prime}}\in\Lambda_{i}}\norm{[h_{X_{r+r'}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r'}}^{\Lambda_i}(n_{r+r'})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_{r+1}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_1}^{\Lambda_i}(n_{r+1})}(\tau),[h_{X_r}^{\gamma_r (n_r)}(\tau), \cdots, [h_{X_2}^{\gamma_2 (n_2)}(\tau),h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}(\tau)]]]]]}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{r+r^{\prime}-1}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}}\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left(\sum_{\gamma_{r^{\prime\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Y_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}\right)\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i};\\
X_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Y_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\norm{h_{X_{r+r''}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r''}}(n_{r+r''})}(\tau)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{r+r^{\prime}-1}f^{n_{1}}g\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left[\left(r+\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}-1}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)k[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})]^{n_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right].

(322)

In the last inequality, we use the fact that
Y_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}
contains at most
rk+\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}<r^{\prime}}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}k
sites since each
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(\tau)
is
qk
-local.
We also use the extensiveness and the strength of time-dependency for the operator
\bar{\Phi}_{q}(\tau)
given by Lemma
C2
.
Let
C_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{n}
denote the number of copies of Eq. (
321
) in the left-hand side of Eq. (
306
).
For instance, we have
C_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{0}\leq(q_{0}-1)!/[r!(q_{0}-r-1)!]\times r^{q_{0}-1-r}\times\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}
for all the possible numbers
r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r}
, as discussed in Eq. (
320
).
Then, using the relation Eq. (
322
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{\mcl{A}_n(\tau) \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) h_X^{\gamma_1}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}C_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{n}2^{r+r^{\prime}-1}g\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left[\left(\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}-1}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)k[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})]^{n_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right].

(323)

We evaluate the above upper bound in a recursive way.
Supposing that the operator
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)\prod_{q=2}^{q_{0}}[\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)]h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)
is expanded by
C_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{n}
copies of Eq. (
321
), we focus on the operator
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}(\tau)\prod_{q=2}^{q_{0}}[\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)]h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)
.
When the index
q_{n+1}
is equal to
1
, it is expanded by
C_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{n}
copies of copies of
