\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{ad}_{\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)}+i\derivative{\tau}\right)\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r+r^{\prime}}\in\Lambda_{i}}[h_{X_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+r^{\prime}})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_{r+1}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+1})}(\tau),[h_{X_{r}}^{\gamma_{r}(n_{r})}(\tau),\cdots,[h_{X_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}(n_{2})}(\tau),h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}(n_{1})}(\tau)]]]]]

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r+r^{\prime}+1}\in\Lambda_{i}}[h_{X_{r+r^{\prime}+1}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{\Lambda_{i}}}(\tau),[h_{X_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+r^{\prime}})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_{r+1}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+1})}(\tau),[h_{X_{r}}^{\gamma_{r}(n_{r})}(\tau),\cdots,[h_{X_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}(n_{2})}(\tau),h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}(n_{1})}(\tau)]]]]]]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+i\sum_{l=0}^{r+r^{\prime}}\sum_{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r+r^{\prime}}\in\Lambda_{i}}[h_{X_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime}}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+r^{\prime}}+\delta_{l,r+r^{\prime}})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_{r+1}}^{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{1}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(n_{r+1}+\delta_{l,r+1})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_{2}}^{\gamma_{2}(n_{2}+\delta_{l,2})}(\tau),h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}(n_{1}+\delta_{l,1})}(\tau)]]]]].

(324)

The second term comes from the Liebnitz rule.
We consider the summation of the norm over
X\ni i
and
\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}
in a similar manner to Eq. (
322
), and then it is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}=1}^{\Gamma}[\text{norm of Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:A_n_plus_1_com})}]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2^{r+r^{\prime}}f^{n_{1}}g\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left[\left(\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}-1}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)k[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})]^{n_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right]\times|Y_{r+r^{\prime}}|g(\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{\Lambda_{i}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{l=0}^{r+r^{\prime}}2^{r+r^{\prime}-1}f^{n_{1}+\delta_{l,1}}g\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}+\delta_{l,r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left[\left(\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}-1}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)k[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})]^{n_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}+\delta_{l,r+r^{\prime\prime}}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left[2\left(r+\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)kg(\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{\Lambda_{i}})+rf+\sum_{r^{\prime\prime}=1}^{r}f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times 2^{r+r^{\prime}-1}f^{n_{1}}g\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left[\left(\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}-1}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)k[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})]^{n_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left[(2kg(\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{\Lambda_{i}})+f)\sum_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}q_{n^{\prime}}\right]2^{r+r^{\prime}-1}f^{n_{1}}g\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{2}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}}^{1}\left[\left(\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}-1}q_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right)k[f(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})]^{n_{r+r^{\prime\prime}}}g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{r^{\prime\prime}}})\right].

(325)

In the last inequality, we use the relation
f(\bar{\Phi}_{q})=qf
by Lemma
C2
.
We also use
r+\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}q_{r^{\prime\prime}}\leq\sum_{n^{\prime}=0}^{n}q_{n^{\prime}}
.
This comes from the fact that
r
and
r^{\prime}
are at most
q_{0}
and
n
respectively so that the corresponding term Eq. (
321
) can appear in
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)\prod_{q=2}^{q_{0}}\{\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\gamma_{q}}^{\Lambda_{i}}(\tau)\}h_{X}^{\gamma_{1}}(\tau)
.

The inequality Eq. (
325
) holds also for
q_{n+1}\neq 1
when we replace
g(\bar{\Phi}_{1}^{\Lambda_{i}})
with
g(\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n+1}}^{\Lambda_{i}})
with similar calculation, while it overestimates the upper bound due to the absence of the time derivative in Eq. (
324
).
In any case, this relation with the common number of the copies
C_{r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r+r^{\prime}}}^{n}
implies the recursive relation,

[\text{r.h.s. of Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:An_D_h_bound_relation}) for $n+1$}]\leq\left([2kg(\bar{\Phi}_{n+1}^{\Lambda_{i}})+f]\sum_{n^{\prime}=0}^{n}q_{n^{\prime}}\right)\times[\text{r.h.s. of Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:An_D_h_bound_relation}) for $n$}].

(326)

By repeating this relation until the bound for
n=0
appears, we obtain

[\text{r.h.s. of Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:An_D_h_bound_relation}) for $n$}]\leq\prod_{n^{\prime}=0}^{n-1}\left([2kg(\bar{\Phi}_{n^{\prime}}^{\Lambda_{i}})+f]\sum_{n^{\prime\prime}=0}^{n^{\prime}-1}q_{n^{\prime\prime}}\right)(q_{0}-1)!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g,

(327)

where we use Eq. (
320
).
This completes the proof of Eq. (
306
).
\quad\square

Proof of Eq. (
307
).—

The proof is parallel to the one for Eq. (
306
).
We first consider the case with
n=0
, i.e., the upper bound on

\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{q^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{q_{0}}=1}^{\Gamma+1}\norm{ \left(\prod_{q=q'+1}^{q_0} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_i}(\tau)\right) \left[ h_X^{\gamma_{q'}}(\tau) , \left(\prod_{q=2}^{q'-1} \bar{\mcl{D}}_{\gamma_q}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right)H_{\gamma_1}^{\Lambda_{i-1}}(\tau)\right]}

(328)

In a similar manner to Eq. (
317
), the above equation is bounded by at most
(q_{0}-1)!/[r!(q_{0}-1-r)!]\times r^{q_{0}-1-r}\times\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}
copies of

\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1},\\
X_{r^{\prime}+1},\cdots,X_{r}\subset\Lambda_{i}\end{subarray}}\norm{[h_{X_{\gamma_r}}^{\gamma_{q_r}(n_r)}(\tau),\cdots,[h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}(\tau),[h_{X_{r'}}^{\gamma_{r'} (n_{r'})}(\tau),[\cdots,[h_{X_3}^{\gamma_{3} (n_{3})}(\tau),h_{X_{2}}^{\gamma_{2} (n_{2})}(\tau)]]]]]}

(329)

with some integers
r,r^{\prime},n_{1},\cdots,n_{r}
satisfying
r^{\prime}<r\leq q_{0}
and
r+n_{1}+\cdots+n_{r}=q_{0}-1
.
This is different from Eq. (
318
) in that the domain
X
containing the site
i
appears in the middle of the nested commutator.
Let
Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}
denote the union
Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}=X_{2}\cup X_{3}\cup\cdots\cup X_{r^{\prime\prime}-1}
for
r^{\prime\prime}=2,3,\cdots,r^{\prime}
, which contains at most
(r^{\prime\prime}-2)k
sites.
Note that it is different from the union
Y_{r^{\prime\prime}}=X\cup X_{2}\cup X_{3}\cup\cdots\cup X_{r^{\prime\prime}-1}
.
Considering the set of the domains
X,X_{2},\cdots,X_{r}
such that the nested commutators among the operators defined on them can survive, Eq. (
329
) is bounded by

\displaystyle[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:com_h_h_ver2})}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{r-1}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}(\tau)}\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi,(r^{\prime\prime}=2,\cdots,r^{\prime}),\\
X\cap(X_{r^{\prime}}\cup Z_{r^{\prime}})\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r}^{r^{\prime}+1}\left\{\sum_{\gamma_{r^{\prime\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Y_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{r-1}\frac{(r-1)!}{r^{\prime}!}(kg)^{r-r^{\prime}}f^{n_{r^{\prime}+1}+\cdots+n_{r}}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}(\tau)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi,(r^{\prime\prime}=2,\cdots,r^{\prime}),\\
X\cap(X_{r^{\prime}}\cup Z_{r^{\prime}})\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}.

The second inequality is obtained by the same calculation as Eq. (
322
), in which we use the extensiveness and the strength of the time-dependency for
r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime}+1,\cdots,r
.

In order to evaluate the upper bound of Eq. (
), we define the quantity
S_{r^{\prime}}
by

S_{r^{\prime}}=\max_{X\subset\Lambda;|X|\leq k}\left(\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi,(r^{\prime\prime}=2,\cdots,r^{\prime}),\\
X\cap(X_{r^{\prime}}\cup Z_{r^{\prime}})\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}\right),

(331)

for
r^{\prime}\geq 2
and compute its upper bound.
The relation
X\cap(X_{r^{\prime}}\cup Z_{r^{\prime}})
implies that there exists a certain domain
X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\in\{X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}\}
such that
X\cap X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\neq\phi
, and hence
S_{r^{\prime}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle S_{r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi,(r^{\prime\prime}=2,\cdots,r^{\prime}),\\
X\cap X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}

(332)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\sum_{\gamma_{2},\cdots,\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\cap X\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\norm{h_{X_{r'''}}^{\gamma_{r'''}(n_{r'''})}(\tau)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi,(r^{\prime\prime}=2,\cdots,r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1),\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}\cap(X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}\cup Z_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1})\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime\prime\prime}+1}^{r^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{i-1};\\
X_{r^{\prime\prime}}\cap Z_{r^{\prime\prime}}\neq\phi\end{subarray}}\norm{h_{X_{r''}}^{\gamma_{r''}(n_{r''})}(\tau)}\right).

For
r^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq 3
, the summation over
X_{2},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}
can be bounded by
S_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}
since the domain
X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}
contains at most
k
sites due to the locality.
The corresponding part for
r^{\prime\prime\prime}=2
is equal to
1
, and hence we set
S_{1}=1
so that the bound by
S_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}
can work for any
r^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
The summation over
X_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}+1},\cdots,X_{r^{\prime}}
in Eq. (
) is evaluated in a similar manner to Eq. (
322
).
As a result, the quantity
S_{r^{\prime}}
is bounded by

\displaystyle S_{r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}}gS_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}\times\prod_{r^{\prime\prime}=r^{\prime\prime\prime}+1}^{r^{\prime}}\left[(r^{\prime\prime}-1)kf^{n_{r^{\prime\prime}}}g\right]=\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{(r^{\prime}-1)!}{(r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1)!}(kg)^{r^{\prime}-r^{\prime\prime}}f^{n_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\cdots+n_{r^{\prime}}}S_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}.

(333)

This results in the recursive relation,

\frac{S_{r^{\prime}}}{r^{\prime}!f^{n_{2}+\cdots+n_{r^{\prime}}}(kg)^{r^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}\sum_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=2}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{S_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}}{(r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1)!f^{n_{2}+\cdots+n_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}}(kg)^{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-1}}

(334)

for
r^{\prime}\geq 2
.
With the initial condition
S_{1}=1
, we can easily show that this implies the satisfaction of
S_{r^{\prime}}\leq r^{\prime}!f^{n_{2}+\cdots+n_{r^{\prime}}}(kg)^{r^{\prime}-1}
by induction.

We go back to the evaluation of Eq. (
).
Substituting
S_{r^{\prime}}\leq r^{\prime}!f^{n_{2}+\cdots+n_{r^{\prime}}}(kg)^{r^{\prime}-1}
into Eq. (
), we arrive at

\displaystyle[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:S_r_intro})}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{r-1}\frac{(r-1)!}{r^{\prime}!}(kg)^{r-r^{\prime}}f^{n_{r^{\prime}+1}+\cdots+n_{r}}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r^{\prime}}=1}^{\Gamma}\sum_{X\ni i}\norm{h_X^{\gamma_1 (n_1)}(\tau)}S_{r^{\prime}}

(335)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(r-1)!(2kg)^{r-1}f^{q_{0}-1-r}g.

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:D_h_D_h})}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{q_{0}-1}\frac{(q_{0}-1)!}{r!(q_{0}-1-r)!}r^{q_{0}-1-r}\Gamma^{q_{0}-1-r}(r-1)!(2kg)^{r-1}f^{q_{0}-1-r}g

(336)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(q_{0}-1)!(2kg+\Gamma f)^{q_{0}-1}g,

which completes the proof of Eq. (
307
) for
n=0
.
The upper bound when the operator
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\tau)
is applied is essentially the same as Eq. (
306
).
The locality, the extensiveness, and the strength of time-dependency appearing in Eq. (
329
) are common with Eq. (
318
).
We can construct the recursive relation for the upper bound like Eq. (
326
).
This results in the inequality, Eq. (
307
).
\quad\square

C.4
Error bounds of time-dependent MPF

Here, we complete the proof of Theorem
17
, which gives the bound on the time-dependent MPF errors.
We prove the following theorem using the results of Lemma
C3
and Lemma
C4
.

Theorem C7
.

(Formal version of Theorem
17
)

Let
\bar{T}(t)=e^{-iHt}+\order{t^{p+1}}
be a
p
th-order time-independent PF for a Hamiltonian
H=\sum_{\gamma=0}^{\Gamma}H_{\gamma}
, defined by Eq. (
72
).
We set the quantities
\{c_{j},k_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
so that the time-independent MPF
\bar{M}(t)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}\{\bar{T}(t/k_{j})\}^{k_{j}}
can satisfy the order condition,
\bar{M}(t)=e^{-iHt}+\order{t^{m+1}}
.
For the time
t
small enough to satisfy

t\leq\min\left(\frac{1}{4e^{3}V_{p}p_{0}(N,\epsilon)(2kg+\Gamma f)},\frac{1}{4V_{p}\max_{\tau\in[0,\tau]}\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)}\right),

(337)

the standard MPF
\bar{N}(t,0)
has an error bounded by

\norm{U(t,0)-\bar{N}(t,0)}\leq\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\left(2V_{p}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\alpha_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)t\right)^{m+1}+\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\|\vec{k}\|_{1}\epsilon.

(338)

Proof.—

We begin the proof with Eq. (
262
), which tells us the error bound by the triangle inequality via the truncated BCH formula.
We have already computed the difference between the standard MPF and its approximation by the truncated BCH formula in Lemma
C3
or Lemma
C4
, and the assumption
t\leq\{4e^{3}V_{p}p_{0}(N,\epsilon)(2kg+\Gamma f)\}^{-1}
in Eq. (
337
) is made for applying them.
The remaining task is to evaluate the norm of

U(t,0)-\sum_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{j}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}|0}=\sum_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{j}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\left\{e^{-iH^{F}t}-[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}\right\}|0}.

(339)

The right-hand side comes from the expression of the time evolution by Eq. (
33
) and the relation
\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}=1
, which is always demanded for the order condition
\bar{M}(t)=e^{-iHt}+\order{t}
.

The power of the truncated BCH formula
[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
expressed by Eq. (
240
) can be seen as a time-evolution operator under the Hamiltonian
H^{F}+\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}t^{q-1}/(k_{j})^{q-1}
.
Regarding
H^{F}
and
\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}t^{q-1}/(k_{j})^{q-1}
respectively as unperturbed and perturbed Hamiltonians in the interaction picture, it can be represented by the Dyson series,

[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}=e^{-iH^{F}t}+e^{-iH^{F}t}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-i)^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\differential\tau_{n}\cdots\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}\differential\tau_{1}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{i\tau_{n^{\prime}}\mathrm{ad}_{H^{F}}}\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}t^{q-1}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}\right).

(340)

This series is absolutely convergent with respect to
n
since each
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
is a Toeplitz matrix given by Eq. (
223
) and its norm is bounded by
\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q^F}\leq\max_{\tau\in[0,T]}(\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(\tau)})<\infty
.
Thus, the difference between
e^{-iH^{F}t}
and
[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}
in Eq. (
339
) is calculated as follows,

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c_{j}\left\{e^{-iH^{F}t}-[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-i)^{n}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{2}}\differential s_{1}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}\sum_{q=p+1}^{p_{0}}\frac{\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}t^{q}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}\right)e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}},

(341)

where we set
s_{n+1}=1
and use
\sum_{j}c_{j}=1
.
We arrange the terms with respect to the time
t
, which results in

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c_{j}\left\{e^{-iH^{F}t}-[\bar{T}_{p_{0}}^{\mathrm{BCH}}(t/k_{j})]^{k_{j}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=-\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-i)^{n}\sum_{q=pn+1}^{(p_{0}-1)n+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+1\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q+n-1\end{subarray}}\frac{t^{q+n-1}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{2}}\differential s_{1}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}\right)e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{q=p+1}^{\infty}t^{q}\sum_{j}\frac{c_{j}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}\sum_{n=\left\lceil\frac{q-1}{p_{0}}\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor\frac{q-1}{p}\right\rfloor}(-it)^{n-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+1\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q+n-1\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{2}}\differential s_{1}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}\right)e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}.

(342)

The calculation in Eqs. (
341
) and (
342
) is exactly the same as that for the time-independent MPF
[
43
]
, in which
H^{\mathrm{LP}},H_{1}^{\mathrm{Add}},\cdots,H_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{Add}}
are associated with
H_{0},H_{1},\cdots,H_{\Gamma}
in the time-independent case via the correspondence of
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
[Eq. (
49
)] and
\bar{T}(t)
[Eq. (
72
)].
The quantities
\{c_{j},k_{j}\}
established for the time-independent MPF
\bar{M}(t)
to satisfy the order condition
\bar{M}(t)=e^{-iHt}+\order{t^{m+1}}
are chosen so that the terms with
q=p+1,p+2,\cdots,m
in Eq. (
342
) can vanish (See the end of Appendix
C.4
for the detailed choice).
Therefore, with the same choice of
\{c_{j},k_{j}\}
, the error for the time-dependent case is expressed by

\displaystyle[\text{Eq. (\ref{EqA_MPF:Evol_BCH_diff})}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{q=m+1}^{\infty}t^{q}\sum_{j}\frac{c_{j}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}\sum_{n=\left\lceil\frac{q-1}{p_{0}}\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor\frac{q-1}{p}\right\rfloor}(-it)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\,\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+1\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q+n-1\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{1}\differential s_{n}\cdots\int_{0}^{s_{2}}\differential s_{1}e^{-il\omega t}\bra{l}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}\right)e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}\right]\ket{0}.

We note that the above series is absolutely convergent with respect to
l
and
q
, which can be easily ensured by the smoothness of the Hamiltonian as we do for Eq. (
272
).
Changing the order of the summation with respect to
l
and
q
, we focus on the operator,

\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\bra{l}\left[\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}\right)e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}\right]\ket{0}.

(344)

We insert the completeness
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\ket{l}\bra{l}=1
and use the translation symmetry of
H^{F}
by Eq. (
36
) and
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
by Eq. (
295
).
This results in the relation,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-il\omega t}\braket{l|\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}\right)e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}|0}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{1},\cdots,l_{n+1}\in\mathbb{Z},\\
m_{1},\cdots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}e^{-il_{n+1}\omega t}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\braket{l_{n^{\prime}+1}|e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}|l_{n^{\prime}}+m_{n^{\prime}}}\braket{l_{n^{\prime}}+m_{n^{\prime}}|\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}|l_{n^{\prime}}}\right)\braket{l_{1}|e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}|0}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l_{1},\cdots,l_{n+1}\in\mathbb{Z},\\
m_{1},\cdots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(e^{-il_{n^{\prime}+1}\omega ts_{n^{\prime}+1}}\braket{l_{n^{\prime}+1}|e^{-iH^{F}t(s_{n^{\prime}+1}-s_{n^{\prime}})}|0}\{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}(t)\}_{m_{n^{\prime}}}e^{-im_{n^{\prime}}\omega ts_{n^{\prime}}}\right)e^{-il_{1}\omega ts_{1}}\braket{l_{1}|e^{-iH^{F}ts_{1}}|0}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left[U(s_{n^{\prime}+1}t,s_{n^{\prime}}t)\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}(s_{n^{\prime}}t)\right]U(s_{1}t,0).

(345)

We use the expression of
\bar{\Phi}_{q}^{F}
by Eq. (
223
) in the second and the last equalities, and use that of the time evolution
U(t,0)
by Eq. (
33
) in the last equality.
The unitary operators
U(s_{n^{\prime}+1}t,s_{n^{\prime}}t)
and
U(s_{1}t,0)
do not affect the norm.
We can use the relation
\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(s_{n'}t)}\leq\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}(\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(\tau)})
since we have
0\leq s_{n^{\prime}}\leq 1
in Eq. (
).
As a result, the norm of Eq. (
339
) is bounded by

\displaystyle U(t,0)-\sum_{j}c_{j}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\norm{U(t,0)-\sum_j c_j \sum_{l \in\bbZ}\braket{l|[ \bar{T}_{p_0}^\mr{BCH}(t/k_j) ]^{k_j}|0}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\sum_{q=m+1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=\left\lceil\frac{q-1}{p_{0}}\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor\frac{q-1}{p}\right\rfloor}t^{q+n-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p+1\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q+n-1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n!}\prod_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left(\norm{\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n'}} (\tau)}\right)

(346)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\sum_{q=m+1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{t^{q+n-1}}{n!}\left(V_{p}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)\right)^{q+n-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\leq p_{0};\\
q_{1}+\cdots+q_{n}=q+n-1\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}}{4}\sum_{q=m+1}^{\infty}\left(2V_{p}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\alpha_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)t\right)^{q}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\left(2V_{p}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\alpha_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)t\right)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\left(2V_{p}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\alpha_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)t\right)^{m+1}.

In the second inequality, we use Lemma
C1
for upper-bounding
\norm{\bar{\Phi}_q(\tau)}
by the quantity
\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},p}(\tau)
and use the definition of the quantity
\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},p}^{\mathrm{MPF}}(\tau)
by Eq. (
166
).
The last inequality comes from the assumption Eq. (
337
).
The error bound Eq. (
338
) is immediately obtained by Eq. (
262
).
\quad\square

Choice of
\{c_{j},k_{j}\}
.—

We discuss the concrete choice of the coefficients
\{c_{j}\}
and the integers
\{k_{j}\}
.
We always impose
\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}=1
so that at least the order condition
\bar{N}(t,0)=e^{-iHt}+\order{t}
can be satisfied.
On the other hand, we demand that the term with
q\leq m
in Eq. (
342
) should vanish for
\bar{N}(t,0)=e^{-iHt}+\order{t^{m+1}}
.
Thus, it is sufficient to find
\{c_{j},k_{j}\}
such that

\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}=1,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{c_{j}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}=0,\quad\text{for $q=p+1,p+2,\cdots,m$}

(347)

is satisfied.
Or if the time-dependent PF
\bar{S}(t,0)
has an even order
p
and the corresponding time-dependent PF
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
satisfies the symmetric condition
\bar{T}^{F}(-t)^{\dagger}=\bar{T}^{F}(t)
, the terms with even
q
in Eq. (
342
) are all zero due to
\bar{\Phi}_{q_{n^{\prime}}}^{F}=0
for even
q_{n^{\prime}}
.
In that case, it is sufficient to impose

\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}=1,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{c_{j}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}=0,\quad\text{for $q=p+1,p+3,\cdots,2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1$}.

(348)

For example, this is the case for the even-order Lie-Suzuki-Trotter formula
\bar{S}_{p}(t,0)
since the time-dependent Lie-Suzuki-Trotter formula
\bar{T}^{F}(t)
, given by Theorem
6
, satisfies the symmetric condition.
The conditions Eqs. (
347
) or (
348
) are exactly the same as those for the time-independent MPF
[
37
]
.
While we focus on the standard MPF
\bar{N}(t,0)
here, they are valid also for the generalized MPF
N(t,0)
.

The well-conditioned MPF is a MPF satisfying
\norm{\vec{c}}_{1},\|\vec{k}\|_{1}\in\mathrm{poly}\left(m\right)
, which can be efficiently implemented on quantum circuits by LCU.
Such a solution is obtained when we consider PFs satisfying the symmetric condition
[
37
]
.
We set
J=m/2
for even
m
and demand

\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}=1,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{c_{j}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}=0,\quad\text{for $q=3,5,\cdots,2J+1$}.

(349)

Although it is redundant by the conditions for
q=3,5,\cdots,p-1
compared to Eq. (
348
), it allows a good choice of
\{k_{j}\}
given by

k_{j}=\left\lceil K\left|\sin\left(\frac{\pi(2j-1)}{8m}\right)\right|^{-1}\right\rceil,\quad\|\vec{k}\|_{1}\in\order{m^2 \log m}.\\

(350)

The scale factor
K<2\sqrt{2}m/\pi
is a number such that
\{k_{j}\}
becomes mutually different integers.
The set of the coefficients
\{c_{j}\}_{j}
is given by

c_{j}=\prod_{i\neq j}\frac{1}{1-(k_{i}/k_{j})^{2}},\quad\norm{\vec{c}}_{1}\in\order{\log m}.

(351)

When we compose of the MPF with PFs which do not necessarily satisfy the symmetric condition, we set
J=m
and demand

\sum_{j=1}^{J}c_{j}=1,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{c_{j}}{(k_{j})^{q-1}}=0,\quad\text{for $q=2,3,\cdots,m$}.

(352)

This reduces to Eq. (
349
) when we replace
k_{j}
with
(k_{j})^{2}
, and hence we can use a squared value of Eq. (
350
) as
k_{j}
.
The choice of
\{c_{j}\}
is just the same as the symmetric case.

Appendix D
PF for non-unitary dynamics

While we concentrate on evaluating the explicit error for time-dependent hermitian Hamiltonians
H(t)
, our results can be easily extended to the case when they are non-hermitian as well as time-independent cases
[
12
]
.
Such cases are useful for solving general linear differential equations
[
8
]
, simulating open quantum systems
[
29
,
27
]
, or preparing thermal states via imaginary time evolution
[
44
]
.

Let us consider a time-dependent Hamiltonian
H(t)=\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}H_{\gamma}(t)
, where each term
H_{\gamma}(t)
is of class
C^{p+2}
but not necessarily hermitian.
We note that, regardless of hermiticity of the Hamiltonian, the decay of the transition amplitude
\braket{l|e^{-iH^{F}t}|0}
and the resulting absolute convergence of Eq. (
33
) are maintained
[
40
]
.
This means that Floquet analysis in the main text holds also for non-hermitian cases.
What differs from hermitian cases is the absence of unitary operators in the error representation.
Reviewing the discussion in Sections
IV
, Eq. (
87
) implies that the error of the standard PF is bounded by

\norm{U(t,0)-\bar{S}(t,0)}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\differential\tau\norm{U(t,\tau)}\cdot\bar{S}(\tau,0)\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\norm{\bar{S}(\tau,0) \sum_{l \in\bbZ} \braket{l|\bar{\Delta}^F(\tau)|0}}.

(353)

The operator
\Delta^{F}(\tau)
is defined by Eq. (
77
) with replacing the conjugate of matrices by their inverse.
The norm of the time-evolution operator
U(t,\tau)
is bounded by

\norm{U(t,\tau)}\leq\exp\left(\int_{\tau}^{t}\differential\tau_{1}\norm{\mr{Im} H(\tau_1)}\right),

(354)

where
\mathrm{Im}H
denotes
(H-H^{\dagger})/2i
.
On the other hand, as shown in Eq. (
109
), the operator
\bar{S}(\tau,0)\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\braket{l|\bar{\Delta}^{F}(\tau)|0}
includes non-unitary operators, each of which has the following norm,

\displaystyle\norm{\bar{S}(\tau,0) \bar{S}_{\prec v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tau,0)^\dagger}\cdot\norm{\bar{S}_{\prec v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tau,0)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\norm{\prod_{(v',\gamma')=(v,\gamma)}^{(V_p,\Gamma)} e^{-i H_{\pi_{v'}(\gamma')}(\beta_{v'\gamma'}\tau)\alpha_{v'\gamma'}\tau}}\cdot\norm{\prod_{(v',\gamma')=(1,1)}^{(v,\gamma-1)} e^{-i H_{\pi_{v'}(\gamma')}(\beta_{v'\gamma'}\tau)\alpha_{v'\gamma'}\tau}}

(355)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(V_{p}\tau\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\norm{\mr{Im}H_\gamma(\tau)}\right),

or

\norm{\bar{S}(\tau,0) \bar{S}_{\prec v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tau,0)^\dagger e^{iH_{\pi_v(\gamma)}(\beta_{v\gamma}\tau)\alpha_{v\gamma}\tau_1}}\cdot\norm{e^{-iH_{\pi_v(\gamma)}(\beta_{v\gamma}\tau)\alpha_{v\gamma}\tau_1} \bar{S}_{\prec v\tilde{\gamma}}(\tau,0)}\leq\exp\left(V_{p}\tau\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\norm{\mr{Im}H_\gamma(\tau)}\right).

(356)

Following the discussion from Eq. (
113
) to Eq. (
121
), we obtain an upper bound,

\displaystyle\bar{S}(\tau,0)\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\norm{\bar{S}(\tau,0) \sum_{l \in\bbZ} \braket{l|\bar{\Delta}^F (\tau)|0}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4(V_{p})^{p+1}\tau^{p}\exp\left(V_{p}\tau\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}\max_{\tau\in[0,t]}\norm{\mr{Im}H_\gamma(\tau)}\right)\max_{\tau_{1}\in[0,\tau]}\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},p}(\tau_{1}).

(357)

Finally, using the relation Eq. (
353
), we arrive at the following error bound for non-unitary dynamics.

Lemma D1
.

(Standard PF error for non-unitary dynamics)

When the Hamiltonian
H(t)
is not necessarily hermitian, the
p
th-order standard PF
\bar{S}(t,0)
has an error bounded by

\norm{U(t,0)-\bar{S}(t,0)}\leq 4(V_{p}t)^{p+1}\exp\left(V_{p}t\sum_{\gamma=1}^{\Gamma}\max_{\tau\in[0,\tau]}\norm{\Im H_{\gamma}(\tau)}+\int_{0}^{t}\differential\tau\norm{\mr{Im}H(\tau)}\right)\max_{\tau\in[0,\tau]}\bar{\alpha}_{\mathrm{com},p}(\tau).

(358)

This bound is parallel to the one for time-independent cases
[
12
]
in that they have additional exponential factors in the time
t
.
While we hereby concentrate on the standard PF for non-unitary dynamics, such extensions of other variants of time-dependent PFs are straightforward.
We note that this extension also applies to the time-dependent MPF.
Namely, the error bounds of the generalized PF or the time-dependent MPFs for non-unitary dynamics can be obtained by upper-bounding the non-unitary time-evolution operators appearing in their error representations.
Such error bounds will have some additional exponential factors like Lemma
D1
.
